<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As we can see, only the values of VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">] matters for the equivalent condition (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Therefore, we focus on constructing VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">]. In addition, we want a continuous</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">calibrated quantile function.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">The case</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1 or</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1 is special, which means that VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is a constant on the tail part. <span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> k</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> k</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">An illustration of the functions</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> on [0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">] in Case 2 to Case 5 is presented in Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 5</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">we omit Case 1 in which</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a constant function on [0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Theorem 1.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∆</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, the random variable</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with a continuous quantile function</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">given by</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> �→</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> satisﬁes</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Remark</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> As we can see from Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 5</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, some parts of the calibrated quantile function may be ﬂat,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">corresponding to the existence of atoms in the distribution. This may be considered as undesirable</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">from a modeling perspective, and indeed it is forced by the boundary cases of (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∆ in</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. The ﬂat parts in Cases 1 to 3 are necessary due to Propositions</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 6</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. On the other hand,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">the ﬂat part in Case 5 can be replaced by a strictly decreasing function. For instance, we can replace</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">the ﬂat part with a strictly decreasing linear segment as long as</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> satisﬁes the bounds shown in</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Propositions</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 7</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> in Appendix</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> A</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Another way is to set VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) as</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> k</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) if</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, and this choice is applied in the numerical examples in the</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Introduction and Section</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 6</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. The interested reader can see Propositions</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 6</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 7</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> in Appendix</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> A</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, where</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">10</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Figure 5: An illustration of</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> in cases 2 to 5</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
