<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 2</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Exp(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/λ</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">GPD(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>



<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ̸</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 0</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>




<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For a given dual PELVE curve</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we ﬁnd the solution to the integral equation by the following</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">steps.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">15</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1. Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2. Find</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> that satisﬁes</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)d</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> yf</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3. By Proposition</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for some</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∼</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> U(0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1) will have the given dual PELVE</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Therefore, we will focus on characterizing</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> from a given</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> : (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] below. Generally, it is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">hard to characterize</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> explicitly. We ﬁrst formulate the problem as an advanced diﬀerential equation,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">which helps us to ﬁnd solutions.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">4.3</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">Advanced diﬀerential equations</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">In this section, we show that the main objective (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) can be represented by a diﬀerential equation.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">The use of diﬀerential equations in computing risk measures has not been actively developed. The only</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">paper we know is</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Balb´as et al.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2017</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) which addresses a diﬀerent problem.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let us recall the integral equation (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) from Section</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 4.2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. For a function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we solve the function</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> : (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> from (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). We represent (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) by an advanced diﬀerential equation using the following</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">steps.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1. Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> yz</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">It is easy to see that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Hence,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is strictly increasing and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">continuous on (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2. Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> be the inverse function of</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. We have that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> : (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1)]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> �→</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] is a continuous and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">strictly increasing function and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3. Replacing</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) in (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), we have</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)d</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s/u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4. Assume</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is continuously diﬀerentiable.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">It is clear that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is continuously diﬀerentiable on</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1)). Hence, we can represent (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) by the following advanced diﬀerential equation</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))) = 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For a given function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> : (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">such that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ω</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> yz</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]. Then, we</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">solve the function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> by the following diﬀerential equation</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) = 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(4)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">If</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for some</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, then</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a strictly increasing and continuous function such that</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Furthermore, if</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is continuously diﬀerentiable, then we can characterize all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> by (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> /u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is a linear advanced diﬀerential equation</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">16</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">which is well studied in the literature. In</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Berezansky and Braverman</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2011</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), it is shown that there</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">exists a non-oscillatory solution for (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">4.4</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">The constant PELVE curve</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">We ﬁrst solve the case that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] and some constant</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1). As we can</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">see from Table</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, the power function and logarithm function have constant</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. If</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λx</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1, we can see that (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> + 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. In this section, we can characterize all the other solutions</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">which can not be expressed as a linear transformation of the power function. That is, we will see that</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">the set</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">{</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is not a location-scale class. Hence, we can answer the question at the beginning of the section; that</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is, in the case the PELVE is a constant, the set</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> {</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> : Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is not a location-scale class.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Theorem 2.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, any</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with quantile function satisfying Assumption</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/c</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> can be written as</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for some</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∼</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> U(0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> satisfying Assumption</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Furthermore, such</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> has the form</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> O</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">where</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is the root of</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> + 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ζ ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> max</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">{</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> O</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a function</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">such that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> lim sup</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> O</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is a constant.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">The proof of Theorem</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is provided in Appendix</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> B</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. As we can see, Theorem</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> characterizes all</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> such that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. If</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">),</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is negative. As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ζ ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0, we can see that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is regularly varying of index</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Hence, one can then consider the Pareto distribution with survival</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">as a representative solution for the tail behavior. An open question is that, in the</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">general case that the PELVE is not necessarily constant, whether all the solutions behave similarly</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">regarding their tail behavior.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Another interesting implication of the theorem and its proof is that one can give a non-trivial</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">solution for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a constant.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Example 3.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1), let (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ, η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) be a solution of</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">17</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Then, the function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, given by</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">sin(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> < y <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(5)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">satisﬁes</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)d</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> yf</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">cy</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) and Assumption</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, where</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> solves (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> + 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ζ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a constant such that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 and 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> < C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ζ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> |</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">|</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">If we take</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 0, we get the simplest power function for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. If</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ̸</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= 0, the solution (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">5</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">not a linear transformation of the power function solution.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let us look at the example where</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 2 for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1], which means</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2 for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As we have seen in Table</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> can be a solution that leads to</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∼</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> U(0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1). Furthermore,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">according to Example</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we can have another solution</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Cy</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">sin(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">where</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">05096,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ζ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0184 and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">15</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4090. In the left of Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 7</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we have depicted</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">the two solutions for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. We can see they are quite diﬀerent when</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> goes to 1. In the right of Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 7</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">we numerically calculate</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Cy</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">sin(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)). We can see its numerical value is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">almost 1/2 and the discrepancy is due to limited computational accuracy.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Figure 7: Non-unique calibrated functions for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2.</span>
























<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">By letting</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">U</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), we get</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 2 for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] and such</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> does not follow the uniform</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">distribution.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">4.5</span>
<span  style="font-size: 11.9552001953125px;">A numerical method</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">In general, it is hard to get an explicit solution to (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). Here we present a numerical method to</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">solve (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). Let us introduce the following process.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">18</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1. Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, ...,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2. For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1), let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> be the solution to (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Let</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ̸</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ξ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(6)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">on [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">3. We can solve the following ODE on [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">]:</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> .</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">4. Now we can repeat step 3 by induction on [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">] for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> n ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1 by solving</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">u</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> .</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">5. In general, the solution for diﬀerential equation</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> P</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Q</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">��</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Q</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)d</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">So, we get the following solution for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">:</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">w</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
