<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">solution</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1; thus</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">39</span>









<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Figure 18: Lambert</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> W</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> function.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">It is important to note that in general an equation like</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> l</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ze</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> has inﬁnite complex roots.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> iη</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0). In that regard, we have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ze</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> iη</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> iη</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) (cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> i</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> i</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> .</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">This implies that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 0, and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)), leading to</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θe</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">or</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">tan (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> exp</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">We plot the curves</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θe</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span>


<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">to ﬁnd out the relation between</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and the</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">real part of the solution in Figure</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 19</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. The</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">-axis is</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and the</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">-axis is</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. The blue curve is associated</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θe</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, which is essentially the principle branch of the Lambert</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> W</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> function. For any</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, one</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">can ﬁnd the real values of the roots by ﬁxing</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. For instance, the green dashed line is associated with</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">12. As one can see, the curves intersect this line in inﬁnite negative values. For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> b</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0),</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">we can see that the real roots are greater than the real part of the complex roots. For more explanation</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">of this, see</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Siewert and Burniston</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1973</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">40</span>










<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Figure 19: The real part of the Lambert</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> W</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> roots.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">41</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Now assume that all the complex solutions for (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">am</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">are</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">i</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">k</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, . . .</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, where (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0) and (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0). Based on the above discussions, we</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > . . .</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> when</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > . . .</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> when</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> > . . .</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> when</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/e.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> σi</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> |</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ, σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">be the set of all complex numbers. Then,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = exp</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> t</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"></span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is a complex solution that solves (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">11</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). It is clear that (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">11</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) still holds for the linear transform of</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Therefore, for any two 2</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ×</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1 vector</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> A</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> B</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> A</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">B</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σt</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σt</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is also a solution to (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">11</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">In</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Bellman and Cooke</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1963</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), it is shown that all complex solutions to (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">11</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) can be represented</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">as follows:</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Putting it in the real-valued context, we have that all real-valued solutions are in the following form:</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> .</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Now let us check</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0. This means that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> {</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> {</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> must satisfy</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = (1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">((</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) cos (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) + (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) sin (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">σ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> < m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> k</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 3, we have lim</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> /e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Therefore, we need</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">42</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">if</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1 or</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 if</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1. That is</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0. Then the solution can be written as</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> O</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">By a change of variable, we get</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> O</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">y</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">where</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ζ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> min</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">{−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1 +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0, we have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ζ ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> max</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">{</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">}</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> C</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0. Also note that since</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> solves (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">am</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">= (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, by replacing</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">m</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), we get (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">α</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> + 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> c</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>




<span  style="font-size: 14.346199989318848px;">C</span>
<span  style="font-size: 14.346199989318848px;">Omitted proofs in Section</span>
<span  style="font-size: 14.346199989318848px;"> 5</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Proof of Proposition</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 4</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Before proving the statements in the proposition, we introduce the following</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">linking function, for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> it is easy to check that Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> satisﬁes Assumption</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> for any</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. The domain of Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">[0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] and its range is [0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">E</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">[</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">])].</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), we have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = VaR</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Hence we have the simple relationship Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε/π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Therefore, Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)). The function Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> yields an association between a point on the PELVE on (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">E</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">[</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">])]</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">and a point on the dual PELVE curve on (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1] with the same value. Furthermore, we have</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">continuous on (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1) as</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) and Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is continuous.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Next, we show the statements (i)-(iv). The equivalence (i) of monotonicity of Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> and that of</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">·</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) follows from Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = Π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) and that Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is increasing.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For (ii), (iii) and (iv), we ﬁrst show that Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is location-scale invariant and shape relevant (in the</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">sense of (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">13</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)). Assume that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a strictly increasing concave function such that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">By Jensen’s inequality and the dual representation of ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">43</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1). This statement can be found in Appendices A.2 in</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Li and Wang</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">2022</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). Therefore,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)))</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ES</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) = Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(13)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Then, we have Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all strictly increasing concave functions:</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For any strictly increasing convex function</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we can take</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">),</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">which is a strictly increasing concave function. Therefore, we have Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all strictly</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">increasing convex functions</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, we have that</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λX</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is both convex and concave. Therefore,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> [0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1]. In conclusion, we have the following results for Γ.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1) For all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> λ ></span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> a</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">, Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(2) Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all strictly increasing concave functions:</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(3) Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) for all strictly increasing convex functions:</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> :</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> →</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> R</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> with</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈ X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Then, we have (ii), (iii) and (iv) from</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> π</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ε/</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Γ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">ε</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">).</span>




<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Proof of Theorem</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 3</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> The idea is to prove that if 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is convex (concave), then</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> �→</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">((1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">is convex (concave) for all</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1). Then, we can get the desired result by Proposition</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 5</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. We will</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">use the following steps to show this statement.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Step 1.</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = log (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) for</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ess-inf(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ess-sup(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)). Then,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a continuous and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">strictly decreasing function and</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0. Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">be the inverse function of</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">. Now, we have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) = 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (ess-inf(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ess-sup(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">X</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">and</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(log(1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">t</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ∈</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (0</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 1)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Therefore,</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log(1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> F</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+ log(1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+ log(1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> p</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Step 2.</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) :=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). We will show that if 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is convex</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(concave), log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)) is convex (concave).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">As</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">e</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">,</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> we have</span>

We have</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> (log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))) = log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> f</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(14)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Then, taking the derivative on both sides of (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">14</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">), we get</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">⇐⇒</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> d</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">d</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Taking a derivative in both sides again, we get</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">−</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">log(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">S</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">))</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) =</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">d</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">1</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">�</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">.</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Then, 1</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">/η</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> is a convex (concave) function means</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩾</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0 (</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">H</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> ⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0), which gives that log</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> g</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) is</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">convex (concave).</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">Step 3.</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">For</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ <</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> 0, let</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) :=</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">s</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">) +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> θ</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">). We are going to show</span>
<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">lim</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> +</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>

<span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">⩽</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> lim</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> </span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">+</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> z</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> −</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;"> G</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">(</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">x</span><span  style="font-size: 9.962639808654785px;">)</span>
